「チャイナショック2.0」に揺れる世界
〜中国の「強すぎる供給⼒」がもたらす構造変化〜
【みずほ経済インサイト】

### Page 2

本資料の要旨
1
１．「チャイナショック2.0」とは何か P2～12
１．「チャイナショック2.0」とは何か P2～12
• 「チャイナショック」とは、中国の「強すぎる供給力」と輸出攻勢が世界の産業・貿易構造を変える現象
• 「2.0」の特徴は、労働集約型産業での競争力を維持したまま、技術・資本集約型産業での競争力を高めていること
• 背景にあるのが中国特有の技術革新モデル。産業集積と規模の経済もあり、中国企業の競争力が格段に向上
２．「チャイナショック2.0」に揺れる欧州とASEAN P13～24
２．「チャイナショック2.0」に揺れる欧州とASEAN P13～24
• 欧州は輸出減少と輸入増加に直面。基幹産業でも中国製品の存在感が高まり、自動車産業を中心に競争が本格化
• ASEANは中国からの中間財調達が輸出産業にとって必須化。ただし、最終財を中心に域内産品との競合は強まる
• 中国製品の浸透を受け、欧州、ASEANとも貿易障壁措置を拡大。中国企業は貿易障壁を意識し、現地生産を志向

３．「チャイナショック2.0」に相対する世界 P25～32
３．「チャイナショック2.0」に相対する世界 P25～32
• 世界は競争と相互依存のジレンマに直面。国内製造業の強化で中国に対抗しつつ、安価な中国製品の輸入も継続
• 中国は、対中規制や追加関税への報復措置をちらつかせつつ、自由貿易体制の維持を図って自国の利益を最大化
• 中国が新興国においてAI経済圏を形成し、世界経済システムを再構築する「チャイナショック3.0」に発展する可能性も
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

2005年 2015年 2024年（％）
「チャイナショック2.0」とは何か――中国の「強すぎる供給力」が世界を変える現象
 中国の「強すぎる供給力」と輸出攻勢が世界の産業・貿易構造を変える「チャイナショック2.0」が進行中
― 中国がWTO加盟を契機に生産・組立拠点として世界貿易体制に組み込まれたのが「チャイナショック1.0」
―「2.0」では、中国企業の競争力と供給力が飛躍的に向上し、先端産業分野で先進国との競合が激化
 「チャイナショック2.0」の特徴は、中国が労働集約型産業での輸出競争力を維持したまま、技術・資本集約型産業での競争
力を高めていること。中国企業は繊維・履物などの軽工業品からハイテク製品まで幅広い分野で輸出を伸ばしている
― 他方、「自立自強」政策による輸入代替の進展と内需の低迷で輸入が停滞。その結果、貿易不均衡が拡大
3
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

「チャイナショック2.0」はなぜ起きたのか――「中国式イノベーション」と製造業の生産性向上
 「チャイナショック2.0」の背景にあるのが「中国式イノベーション」。新興分野で中国の民営企業が躍進
― 「中国式イノベーション」とは、国家の戦略的主導と民間の市場志向が組み合わさった技術革新モデル（筆者定義）
― その土台にあるのが産業集積と規模の経済。こうした環境下、中国企業の技術力とコスト競争力が格段に向上
 中国の製造業は、政府の産業支援も背景に積極的な設備投資を行い、自動化と生産の効率化が急速に進展
― 産業用ロボットの新規導入台数（2025年）は35.4万台と世界（60.3万台）の過半を占め、稼働台数でも群を抜く
― 世界経済フォーラム（WEF）が認定するライトハウス（先進工場）拠点数で中国は最多。特に生産性の評価が高い

4
「中国式イノベーション」の特徴（筆者定義） 産業用ロボット稼働台数（左）とライトハウス拠点数（右）

製造業付加価値は世界の3割弱を占める規模。貿易構造が高度化しつつある様子も
 中国はすでに「世界の工場」の地位を確立。製造業付加価値（2025年）は4.8兆ドルと、世界の3割弱を占める規模
― 中国の輸出額（2025年）は3.77兆ドルと、世界の14.4％を占める。貿易黒字も1.18兆ドルと圧倒的な大きさ
― 輸出総額だけでなく、技術集約的なハイテク製品（航空、コンピューターなど）の輸出額も8,250億ドルと世界一
 加えて、近年は貿易構造が消費財（最終財）中心から中間財や資本財へと高度化しつつある様子もうかがえる
― より付加価値の高い製品の貿易特化係数（輸出入バランスから貿易品目の国際競争力を示す指標）が上昇傾向
5

軽工業品の高い競争力を維持しつつ、技術力が必要な化学や機械でも競争力向上

 中国は、軽工業品の高い競争力を維持しつつ、技術力が必要な化学や機械などでも競争力を向上させている
― HSコード別の貿易特化係数を見ると、2025年は2005年と比較して輸出競争力が大幅に向上している品目が多い
― 他方で、食品・原材料・素材の多くは輸入に頼る。「世界の工場」における需要拡大や食の多様化などが背景に
6

類
似
度
高
中国と先進国との輸出競合度が急上昇。自動車が最大の上昇要因
 中国と先進国の輸出類似度指数（ESI）は「チャイナショック2.0」で急上昇。両者の競合が強まっていることを示唆
― 日本は「1.0」と「2.0」の時期に、EUは2010年代中盤から「2.0」の時期にかけて、ESIが顕著に上昇
― 実際、EUは「2.0」の「震度」が大きく、中国製品の浸透による製造業の衰退が最も懸念されている地域
― 産業別に見ると、「2.0」では日本、EUともに自動車が最大の上昇要因になっていることが分かる
10

①チャイナショック2.0とは何か

### Page 12

「チャイナショック2.0」のもう一つの特徴は輸入の停滞。背景に「自立自強」の進展
 「チャイナショック2.0」のもう一つの特徴は輸入の停滞。「自立自強」による輸入代替（国産化）の進展が背景に
― かつて加工貿易が主流であった頃は、輸出製品を生産するため中間財や資本財の輸入が増え、先進国にも恩恵
― 世界の輸出入に占める中国のシェアを見ると、輸出が「1.0」の頃からほぼ一貫して上昇しているのに対し、輸入は2010
年代に入って頭打ちとなっていることが分かる
― 世界の輸入に占める中国のシェアを品目別に見ても、2024年は多くの業種で2015年からシェアが縮小傾向にある

### Page 22

ASEAN5：国産品・中国産品の調達額変化の比較
ASEAN：一方、中国産品とASEAN域内産品の競合は強まる傾向
21
 素材や消費財、機械・電気機器等では、中国製品がASEAN域内産品を圧迫。最終需要で競合する分野が多い傾向
― 上記分野では中国産品の供給増と域内産品の供給減が同時に発生。中国産品が域内産品を上回って増加する例も
― 中間需要は3分野が該当。一方、最終需要では5分野が該当し、消費財等の部門でより強く競合している可能性を示唆
（注） ADBの国際産業連関表（実質ベース、2010年基準）をASEAN5の国際産業連関表へと加工し、中間投入・最終需要品それぞれにおける国産品・中国産品の金額を求めたもの
（出所） ADBより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

22
 EUは中国製品の流入拡大を背景に、貿易措置を積極化
― 対中貿易措置は2023年以降に増加。化学品や鉄鋼など幅広い品目が対象。2024年には中国製EVへの追加関税を発

― 一方、直近ではドイツも保護主義路線へと徐々にシフト。背景には主力産業である自動車業界による路線変更
 ASEANも貿易保護措置を拡大する傾向。鉄鋼製品等へのアンチダンピング・セーフガードのほか、越境ECへも対応を拡大
― ただし、対中貿易措置の比率は目立って高まらず。外交関係への配慮から、中国以外の地域も含む形で規制を強化

「チャイナショック2.0」の光と影――安価な製品供給の恩恵と先進国の競争力喪失リスク
 中国の「強すぎる供給力」は、世界市場に低価格と安定供給という恩恵をもたらしている
― 中国の輸出価格が相対的に低下する一方、輸出数量はハイペースで拡大しており、輸出ドライブが鮮明に
― 輸出ドライブによる安価な中国製品の供給拡大が世界の財価格を押し下げ、インフレ圧力の緩和にも寄与
 他方で、中国の高い輸出競争力は輸入国の産業競争力の低下と対中依存度の上昇を招いていると考えられる

― 先進国が中国企業の技術革新力とコスト競争力に圧倒され、得意としてきた先端産業で競争力を喪失するリスク
― 関税などで対中輸入を抑制しても、供給網を通じた中国依存は容易に低下せず。経済安保上の脆弱性は残存
26

（出所） CPB、LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 28

「チャイナショック2.0」への対応――国内製造業の強化で対抗しつつ、中国製品の輸入も継続
 世界は競争と相互依存のジレンマに直面。国内製造業の強化で中国に対抗しつつ、安価な中国製品の輸入も継続
― 先進国は、戦略分野での産業基盤の強化や対中デリスキングを図りつつ、非機微分野で貿易・投資活動を維持
― 新興国は、脱中国化を図る供給網の一部を取り込んで国内産業の育成を企図。過剰な中国製品の流入には警戒も
 中国は、対中規制や追加関税への報復措置をちらつかせつつ、自由貿易体制の維持を図って自国の利益を最大化
― 過剰生産能力への批判を一蹴し輸出拡大。一方で、関税回避や規制対応のため、海外での現地生産も積極展開
27
「チャイナショック2.0」への対抗と協調

### Page 29

EUは産業競争力の回復に向け、政策ロードマップを策定。IAAにより域内調達も強化へ
 第二期フォンデアライエン体制下で、EUは「競争力回復」に向けた政策のロードマップ（競争力コンパス）を策定
― （１）米中とのイノベーション格差是正、（２）脱炭素化と競争力強化の両立、（３）過剰な域外依存の軽減と安全保障の強
化を目指す。加えて、市場統合の深化や規制緩和等を内容とする（４）分野横断的取り組みを実行する構え
 競争力コンパス枠組みのもと、欧州は原産地規則の強化により、EVや鉄鋼産業等の域内調達を強力に推進する方針
― 2026年3月以降、欧州委員会は域内調達の拡大に向け、IAA（産業加速法）の立法化に着手
― IAAの現行案では、EVの公共調達や補助金等の支援に対し、域内調達・組立の要件を導入予定
（注） 7要件：①実質的な内国民待遇、②経済安全保障、③競争条件＋ILO、④パリ協定＋炭素価格、⑤企業行動・人権、⑥低炭素需要政策、⑦迂回防止
（出所） 欧州委員会（2025）“A Competitiveness Compass for the EU(factsheet)”、ほか各種資料より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 31

政策の概要国
• 2026年9月、輸入電気自動車への物品税引上げを決定。バッテリーEVのうち国内生産がなく、国産部品も使
用していない車両には2027年から物品税を10％→35％に引き上げる計画
• 現地部品の使用など、一定の国内調達や今後の調達率引き上げが見込まれるものについては、税率引き下
げも検討
タイ
• 四輪電気自動車（BEV）について国産化率規定を設置。政府は2026年までに40％、2030年以降に80％と段
階的な基準値引き上げを目指す
• 完成車輸入優遇の利用企業に、2027年末までに輸入台数以上の国内生産を義務付け
インドネシア
• 2026年7月、EVの国内生産・部品製造を支援するEVインセンティブ戦略（EVIS）を導入。交付された納税証
明書を各種納税に充当することで、企業が税務負担を軽減できる仕組み
• 一定の投資・生産要件を満たす企業に対し、投資額の最大40％を支給
• 国内製造・組立車両の生産実績に応じ、工場出荷価格の最大12％相当を支給
フィリピン
• 国内組立EVや関連部品の税金を2027年末まで一部免除（EV本体：物品税・販売税、部品：輸入関税）

• 完成車輸入に車両価格、およびエンジン容量の下限を設定（2026年7月～）マレーシア
• 国内で製造・組立を行い、生産台数等の要件を満たす企業に、国内生産できない自動車部品の輸入関税を
免除。環境対応車も対象ベトナム
ASEANは、対中関係深化のメリットを維持すべく、自動車を中心に現地生産を促す
30
 ASEANは現地生産要件の設置などで、中国との貿易関係や中国企業の投資流入を維持しながら、輸入品との競合による
域内産業への圧力緩和を目指す方向
― 2025年以降、ASEAN5各国は、自動車を中心に現地生産の促進や国内規定を徐々に厳格化
― 輸入車を中心にEVの浸透が進む中、現地生産化の促進により、中国企業による域内投資や地場サプライヤー・雇用へ
の波及効果を得る狙い

おわりに：「チャイナショック2.0」とAI――製造業へのAI実装で一段と高まる中国の競争力
 中国は、AI技術で世界をリード。オープンウェイト戦略と国家主導のガバナンスで独自のAIエコシステムを構築しつつある
― 大規模言語モデルの性能は米国に肉薄。コストパフォーマンスで海外にも普及し、トークン消費量のシェアが急拡大
― 同時に、ヒューマノイドや自動運転といったフィジカルAI分野でも、技術開発と社会実装が急ピッチで進んでいる
 中国は今後、製造業へのAI実装によって生産性を向上、技術革新を加速させ、競争力を一段と高めるとみられる
― 中国政府は、経済社会の幅広い分野でAI導入を図る「AI＋（プラス）」政策を推進。「AI＋製造業」により研究開発設計、
生産、物流、サービスなど各バリューチェーンへの実装が進めば、「強すぎる供給力」がさらに強力に

「チャイナショック3.0」の可能性――中国がAI経済圏を形成し、世界経済システムを再構築

 「チャイナショック3.0」は、中国がグローバルサウスにおいてAI経済圏を形成し、世界経済システムを再構築する現象
― 中国は独自の「AIエコシステム」の海外展開でAI経済圏を形成し、既存の技術秩序と産業・貿易構造を変容させる
― また、「世界AI協力機構（WAICO）」などを通じて国際協調を図り、AI経済に対応した標準・制度作りを主導する
― AIを軸とした「3.0」では、モノに留まらない形で中国の存在感が強まる可能性。世界にさらなる変化への対応を迫りうる

eMemory Technology Inc  | Asia Pacific
Security demand resilient amid
cloud slowdown
Record earnings: 3Q revenue was NT$1.21bn (+10.4% Q/Q, +27.1% Y/Y) and
attributable net income was NT$639mn (+10.2% Q/Q, +31.2% Y/Y), 2.7% and 1.6%
above MSe, respectively. Operating income beat MSe by 7.0%, while margin reached
63.2%, 2.6ppt above MSe. Royalties rose 15.4% Q/Q, with 12-inch royalties up 20.2%,
versus 3.5% opex growth. PUF security revenue grew 213% Y/Y to 14.5% of sales.

EPS was NT$8.56.
Royalty outlook: Management expects growth to accelerate on higher advanced-
node rates, product ramps and PUF adoption. Customers favor fewer, higher-value
flagship designs, with advanced-node licensing and royalty values several times
those of mature nodes. Management provided no quantified 4Q guidance.
Security expansion: PUFrt adoption in Arm Neoverse CSS V3, BMC, SSD and
networking broadens data-center exposure. Penetration, content per chip and node

migration support growth, while PQC adds license value. Some 3nm designs are in
production, with 2nm qualification and initial adoption targeted for 2027. PUF-
integrated 1T Flash remains in foundry development, with no launch date. PUF
accounts for only 1.8% of royalties, leaving upside dependent on conversion to
production.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
eMemory Technology Inc (3529.TWO)
We derive our price target from a residual income model. We assume an 8.6% cost of equity
(2.0% risk-free rate, 6.0% equity risk premium, and 1.1 beta), a payout ratio of 90%, a medium-
term earnings growth rate of 23.0%, and a terminal growth rate of 4.4%. Based on these
assumptions, our RI model implies an equity value of NT$249bn, or NT$3,330 per share.
Risks to Upside

n Faster-than-expected PUF adoption
n Stronger-than-expected advanced-node OTP migration
n Higher-than-expected royalty expansion from wafer value growth
Risks to Downside
n Slower-than-expected adoption by key customers
n Slower-than-expected PQC adoption
n Increasing geopolitical and trade policy risks

### Page 1

Merida (9914.TW)
3Q Briefing Takeaway – New Model Cycle to Drive 2027 Recovery; SBC 
Visibility Remains Limited 
CITI'S TAKE 
We expect Merida’s core business to recover in 2027E, supported by 
accelerating new model launches and largely normalized EU inventories. 
However, we expect the recovery in 2027E to be gradual with US demand 
remaining sluggish. Meanwhile, despite improving pre-tax profits, SBC’s 
profitability remains highly uncertain. We trim our TP to NT$67 factoring in 
a lowered NTM P/E of 11x and earnings cuts. Remain Neutral as we see 
limited near-term catalysts and prefer to await clearer evidence of 
sustainable SBC profitability and stronger core earnings momentum.
Outlook: More new model launches in 2027E to drive a sales comeback — Mgmt. 
attributed softer shipments this year to deliberate reductions in existing models 
ahead of more new product launches in 2027E. Both Merida’s own brand and its 
major OEM customer, SBC, have adopted this strategy, aiming to preserve pricing 
discipline and avoid excessive discounting. Mgmt. expects the inventory 
adjustment to conclude by year-end and most new models are scheduled to begin 
shipping from Jan-27, paving the way for a sales recovery in 2027E.  
EU leads the recovery; China stabilizes while the US remains sluggish — Merida’s 
own brand grew 14% y/y in 9M26 in EU, supported by solid demand for road and 
gravel bikes. The mgmt. believe inventory normalization is largely complete in EU. 
China’s own brand sales grew 1% y/y in 9M26, better than the original expectation 
of DD% decline, helped primarily by entry and mid end bikes. Mgmt. expects another 
single digit sales growth in China in 2027E, supported by resilient commuter 
demand. The recovery of US market, on the other hand, is lagging EU and China amid 
inflationary pressure and persisting macro uncertainties.   
Still low visibility on SBC profitability —The weak bottom-line of SBC was primarily 
due to a conservative effective tax rate assumptions rather than a deterioration of 
its fundamentals. SBC’s 9M26 pre-tax profits grew 42% y/y or +20% y/y excluding 
US tariff refunds, reflecting improving operating performance and cost discipline. 
While improving pre-tax profitability is encouraging, we remain cautious on SBC’s 
near-term earnings contribution until there is a greater clarity on its normalized 
effective tax rate and the sustainability of US profitability. (continued)

Earnings revisions and implications — We lower our earnings estimates by 20%
for 2026E and 6% for 2027E/2028E, mainly reflecting weaker-than-expected core
sales and profitability, alongside continued uncertainty surrounding SBC’s
profitability. We cut our TP to NT$67 based on 11x 4Q26-3Q27E P/E, down from
15.5x, factoring in weaker-than-expected core business and low visibility of SBC.
While we acknowledge the potential for a stronger sales recovery in 2027E as

Merida accelerates its new model launches, we believe the recovery will be gradual
rather than a meaningful acceleration in the underlying market demand.
Meanwhile, SBC’s uncertain net profitability and lingering US market weakness
remain the key overhang.  We would prefer to stay on the sidelines, awaiting clearer
evidence of sustainable SBC net profitability and stronger earnings momentum
from the core business. Reiterate Neutral.

Bull/Bear: Merida (9914.TW)

### Page 5

Merida (9914.TW)
17 November 2026 Citi Research
5
Merida
Company description
Merida is the second-largest bike manufacturer in Taiwan. Founded in 1972,
Merida was initially involved in OEM manufacturing before it started
marketing bikes under its own Merida brand in 1986. Merida has expanded its
footprint globally through its Merida brand and three OEM brands:
Specialized (in which Merida has a 35% stake), Centurion (51% stake), and
Miyata (45%). Merida's own brand contributes around 30-40% of total

revenues. Merida has established a proprietary network of stores throughout
China, with 16 wholly owned subsidiaries and close to 2,000 retailers that sell
only Merida-branded bikes.
Investment strategy
We rate Merida shares as Neutral. Although bike inventories have normalised
in the major markets after aggressive clearance over the past 2 years, slower-
than-expected bike demand recovery continues to weigh on Merida's topline
momentum. The EU market appears to be where we should see better growth

for 2026E, while China market also turns better than expected from 2Q26; yet
with low visibility of SBC profit trend and limited positive catalysts in sight,
we expect the share price to be range bound.
Valuation
Our target price for Merida shares of NT$93 is based on a 4Q26-3Q27E P/E of
11x, a 50% discount to its historical average of 22x during pre-Covid years
(2014-2019), in view of soft order momentum as a result of slow recovery in
bike demand, a volatile margin trend, and low visibility of SBC.

Risks
Downside risks that could mean the Merida shares trade below our target
price include: 1) e-bike adoption slower than expected; 2) weaker restocking
demand; 3) lukewarm sell-through at SBC; and 4) a weaker macro.
Upside risks that could mean the Merida shares trade above our target price
include: 1) e-bike adoption faster than expected; 2) stronger restocking
demand; 3) stronger sell-through at SBC; and 4) a strong macro.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

公司簡介 
上品為從事氟素樹脂材料相關製品之生產
廠商，主要經營氟素樹脂材料（鐵氟龍）相

生產及銷售，下游應用廣泛，是全球氟素樹
脂加工應用廠商中少數能同時滿足各階段
設備、製造、研發、創新、設計等完整服務
的企業。3Q26 產品營收比重，氟素內襯設
備營收占 69.7% ， 氟 素 內 襯 管 件 類 占
10.2%，氟素應用材料類占 16.5 %，氟素

別區分，台灣占 47.4%，中國占 48.2%，美

主要競爭對手：

葉家宏 bill.yeh@sinopac.com 
本次報告：法說會

上品 (4770 TT) 
在手訂單金額提升，持續擴充產能因應

永豐觀點 
上品在手訂單金額提升至 80 億元，隨著先前低毛利訂單陸續消化完畢，產品組合
預期改善，公司規劃於 2027 持續進行產能擴充，後市展望持續正向，研究部維持
正向看待上品獲利表現。 
 投資評價與建議 
投資建議維持買進，主要係因：上品在手訂單金額持續提升至 80 億元，反映台灣
及中國地區接單表現持續暢旺，隨著公司低毛利訂單出貨完畢，產品組合應逐步好
轉，且公司規劃於 2027 持續擴充產能，挹注未來營收動能，研究部預估公司
2026/2027 稅後 EPS 14.55/23.01 元，參考歷史 PER 區間約位於 10-20X，以
2027 年預估 EPS，給予 15X PER 評價，目標價 345 元，投資建議維持買進。

ESG 評析 
上品企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B+等與 B+等。

### Page 2

2

上品為從事氟素樹脂材料相關製品之生產廠商：公司成立於 1981 年，主要經營氟
素樹脂材料（鐵氟龍）相關製品，包括氟素原料加工之半成品及成品生產及銷售，
下游應用廣泛，是全球氟素樹脂加工應用廠商中少數能同時滿足各階段設備、製
造、研發、創新、設計等完整服務的企業。3Q26 產品營收比重，氟素內襯設備營
收占 69.7%，氟素內襯管件類占 10.2%，氟素應用材料類占 16.5 %，氟素工程收
入占 3.7%；3Q26 公司產品依應用類別區分，半導體占營收 95.1%，化工應用占
比為 3.1%，面板及太陽能應用占 1.4%，其他應用占 0.4%，3Q26 公司營收按地
區別區分，台灣占 47.4%，中國占 48.2%，美國占 0.5%，其他亞洲占 1.6%，歐
洲占 2.2%。 
3Q26 EPS 4.27 元，優於預期：上品 3Q26 營收 16 億元（+22.5% QoQ，+14.4% 
YoY），優於預期，主因上品於 2Q26 擴產，並持續拉升產能利用率，推升營收表
現，毛利率 34.64%（-1.91pts. QoQ，+4.91pts. YoY），低於預期，主因上品出
貨較多低毛利訂單，影響產品組合所致，稅後淨利 34.1 億元（+37.2% QoQ，
+39.9% YoY），優於預期，其中，營業費用低於預期，主因認列預期信用損失金
額降低所致，3Q26 稅後 EPS 4.27 元，優於預期。

### Page 3

3

在手訂單金額持續提升：上品在手訂單金額達 80 億元，較前次發報告時 75 億元
持續提升，其中，約 75%為彰濱廠訂單，約 25%為嘉興廠訂單，預期到年底在手
訂單金額將持續再提升。2026 接單槽車數量達 1,100 台，反映台灣化學品廠因應
晶圓廠大客戶需求而擴充產能，對於上品內襯設備及內襯管件需求同步增溫，而中
國記憶體廠及化學品廠亦持續擴充，下單動能有所增溫。隨著市況於 2026 大幅改
善，彰濱廠及嘉興廠已可挑選毛利率較佳的訂單進行承接，產品組合預期將逐步好
轉，有利其毛利率逐步回升。 
2027 上品計畫持續擴充產能：因應客戶需求強勁，公司於 2Q26 進行擴產，並持
續招工補充人力，拉升產能利用率，目前彰濱廠及嘉興廠已達近滿載水準，公司年
初彰濱廠槽車約當產能約為月產能 80 台，近期已提升至月產能 130 台，預期年底
將達滿載規模月產能 140 台，公司規劃將於 6M27 再擴充至月產能 180 台，嘉興
廠亦規劃提升產能約 30%，以因應客戶強勁需求。而先前承接的低毛利訂單仍有
部分尚未出貨完畢，預期將於 1Q27 出貨完畢，因營收比重持續下降，預期產品組
合應持續改善，有利毛利率及獲利表現回升。

後市展望正向，投資建議維持買進：展望後市，台灣化學品廠及中國記憶體、化學
品廠擴建，推升對上品氟素樹脂產品需求，上品在手訂單金額持續提升，隨著上品
先前承接低毛利訂單陸續出貨完畢，產品組合應逐步改善，推升毛利率及獲利表現
回升。上品規劃於 2027 持續進行產能擴充，挹注未來營收動能，且已可挑選毛利
率較佳的訂單進行承接，後市獲利展望正向。研究部預估公司 2026 營收 56.9 億
元（+20.3% YoY），毛利率 35.1%，稅後淨利 11.6 億元（+41.6% YoY），稅後
EPS 14.55 元，預估 2027 營收 76.8 億元（+35.1% YoY），毛利率 38.31%，稅
後淨利 18.4 億元（+59.2% YoY），稅後 EPS 23.01 元，參考歷史 PER 區間約位
於 10-20X，以 2027 年預估 EPS，給予 15X PER 評價，目標價 345 元，維持買進
投資建議。

.
偏鹰数据与鸽派加息预期
——8月美国CPI数据解读及9月FOMC前瞻
高翔（投资咨询证号： Z0016413 ）
潘响（投资咨询证号： Z0021448 ）
联系邮箱： panxiang@nawaa.com
交易咨询业务资格：证监许可【 2011 】 1290 号
2026 年 9 月 13 日
摘要：在本次数据发布前，市场已经就“核心CPI”是本次唯一关注焦点达成共识。本次核心CPI环比超出预
期的表现实际上是一个偏鹰的结果，这也导致数据发布后市场9月加息预期蹿升。但另一方面，其他大类资产
的表现却显得不够鹰派，周五夜盘的走势呈现出明显的加息前置交易特征，这也是目前市场普遍认为本次会
议可能呈现出“鸽派加息”的原因。推高核心通胀超预期的因素完全集中于核心服务项的异常跳升，主要包
括无线电话服务、酒店住宿，以及受油价传导影响的机票价格。拉动本次核心CPI环比上行的上述关键分项大
概率不具备持续性，剔除上述高波动、一次性扰动因素后，美国底层粘性通胀仍在温和降温，通胀向2%目标
收敛的内生趋势并未被破坏。

9月FOMC前瞻：鸽派加息之后就是一路坦途吗？
1）加息前置交易与鸽派加息预期。我们认为本次会议可能是开启加息的最佳时间窗口，市场的走势也表现出
来了对本次加息的预期。
2）可能情景猜想。最理想情况下，若9月FOMC会议在加息一次的情况下，给出继续观望或者再多一次左右
的加息预期，会出现对市场稳定以及风险偏好回升最为友好的情况。 但我们认为实际情况可能不会如此乐
观，加息落地后大概率进入到新的观察周期，短期内风险资产压力减弱，但依旧会受到地缘以及后续数据公
布情况的影响。
而如果本次会议依旧保持利率水平不变，那并不意味着通胀压力可控，反而可能导向最坏的局面。即便沃什
能够说服其他委员同事放弃抬高利率，从过去两次FOMC会议后的市场表现来看，市场会通过远端利率进一
步的冲高来预防通胀脱锚的风险。
1、为什么本次通胀数据是决定性的？
在本次数据发布前，市场已经就“核心CPI”是本次唯一关注焦点达成共识。理由在于面对超长端利率失控以
及市场愈发高涨的通胀担忧下，沃什在杰克逊霍尔央行年会上指出当前劳动力市场符合充分就业，美联储当

前的首要关注应当是物价。“必须确信潜在通胀正在向我们的目标移动——清晰地、以足够的速度。否则，
我们就还有工作要做。”除此之外，联储理事沃勒在公开讲话时给出了十分清晰的阈值——核心CPI环比
0.2%-0.3%。0.2%这一门槛之所以至关重要，是因为该水平能够确认通胀放缓的实质性趋势。若核心CPI环

比能够稳定在0.2%及以内，意味着该指标将连续4个月处于0.2%以下区间，近7个月平均增速约0.2%（对应
同比约2.4%）。这可以基本确立通胀放缓的趋势，证明该下行信号并非单纯来自季节性扰动。而本次核心
CPI的超预期上行，是推动市场美联储加息预期显著升温的重要因素。
因此本次核心CPI环比超出预期的表现实际上是一个偏鹰的结果，这也导致数据发布后市场9月加息预期蹿
升。但另一方面，其他大类资产的表现却显得不够鹰派，周五夜盘的走势呈现出明显的加息前置交易特征，
这也是目前市场普遍认为本次会议可能呈现出“鸽派加息”的原因。
CPI 数据公布前后市场加息预期
数据来源：彭博  南华研究
2、数据解读
2026年8月美国CPI数据呈现“表观反弹、核心降温”的结构性特征。整体CPI环比升至0.4%（前值0.1%），
同比持平于3.4%；核心CPI环比略超预期升至0.3%（前值0.2%），但同比继续回落至2.4%（前值2.5%）。
本次通胀数据基本符合市场预期，但更关键的是核心CPI环比略超市场预期，这意味着当前核心通胀是否存在

反弹失速的风险吗？
1）推高核心通胀超预期的因素完全集中于核心服务项的异常跳升，主要包括无线电话服务、酒店住宿，以及
受油价传导影响的机票价格。无线电话服务是本月最大的单项拉动项，环比大幅飙升至5.9%，创下历史极
值，该项对核心CPI环比的增量贡献达0.08个百分点左右。外宿分项环比由7月的-3.4%大幅反弹至0.5%，直
接带动整体住房项环比升至0.3%。运输服务中的机票分项受航空燃油价格暴涨直接冲击，机票价格环比跳
涨，带动运输服务环比上行至0.5%。
核心服务部分分项异常调升（ % ）

2）拉动本次核心CPI环比上行的上述关键分项大概率不具备持续性。电话、酒店分项的跳升属于暂时性扰
动：无线电话服务的极端上涨，主要源于美国劳工统计局（BLS）调整统计方法（新算法对全行业套餐调价更
敏感），叠加运营商集中提价，属于典型一次性扰动。酒店住宿价格反弹，主要反映暑期出行需求带来的季
节性扰动。而作为决定通胀中长期走势的核心基石，粘性住房租金已经出现改善。主要居所租金和业主等价
租金环比均从0.3%放缓至0.2%。
综上，剔除上述高波动、一次性扰动因素后，美国底层粘性通胀仍在温和降温，通胀向2%目标收敛的内生趋
势并未被破坏。
CPI 数据整体表现情况（ % ）
数据来源：同花顺  南华研究
3、9月FOMC前瞻：鸽派加息之后就是一路坦途吗？
1）加息前置交易与鸽派加息预期
我们认为本次会议可能是开启加息的最佳时间窗口。首先，目前通胀最大的外生变量来自油价和地缘局势，
而这并不是货币财政政策能够干预的范畴，且由于美国政府高度的不确定性，其未来的走势也具有高度的不

可预测性，这会对通胀带来持续性的压力。其次，长端利率持续上行正在成为风险资产的压力，而缓解这一

### Page 4

压力最好的办法或许是加息。最后则回到联储，在8月杰克逊霍尔年会后，当市场观察到通胀数据表现出脱离
下行趋势的风险时，投资者期待美联储能够履行其政策框架以恢复联储的信誉。
我们认为市场的走势也表现出来了对本次加息的预期。本轮数据发布后，在加息预期显著升温的背景下，美
元指数、美债收益率均走出先上行后快速回落的行情。以10年期美债收益率为例，周五晚间偏鹰数据落地瞬
间，利率直线冲高，但随后又立马180度折返向下，这主要是因为市场事前对于9月加息的计价可能已经比较
充分（周四PPI后利率总体上行10bp以上）。如前文所说，这是典型的加息前置交易特征——更早的加息时
点、更小的加息幅度、更平稳的风险偏好。而上述对于本轮加息的高接受度最终指向了一次鸽派加息落地的
联储会议，即投资者预期本次会议在如期宣布进行加息25bp外，同时可能会伴随出现一份不太激进的政策声
明或是点阵图。
数据公布后， 10Y 美债收益率表现情况（ % ）
数据来源：同花顺  南华研究
2 ）可能情景猜想
最理想情况下，若 9 月 FOMC 会议在加息一次的情况下，给出继续观望或者再多一次左右的加息预期，会出现

对市场稳定以及风险偏好回升最为友好的情况。因为其一方面消除了市场对于联储的质疑，有助于降低通胀
的不确定性，也把加息预期控制在市场可以接受的程度内。从 FED watch 的年内加息预期来看，年内加息 2 次
的概率大概在 70% 左右，而从目前 1y1y 远期利率对 1 年期美债利率的利差来看，短端利率计价了 2 次的降息，
而目前海外投行预期加息次数的众数也在 2 次，总体来说资产的计价已经相对充分。
但我们认为实际情况可能不会如此乐观，加息落地后大概率进入到新的观察周期，短期内风险资产压力减
弱，但依旧会受到地缘以及后续数据公布情况的影响。首先，根据历史经验，美联储利率周期从降息转向加
息后基本会进行多次加息，仅进行一次加息又再度转向降息的案例出现在 1997 年，原因是亚洲金融危机的爆
发。其次，根据沃什本人表态，目前的政策利率水平并不处在限制性的区间，而一次 25bp 的加息或许并不足
够。
而如果本次会议依旧保持利率水平不变，那并不意味着通胀压力可控，反而可能导向最坏的局面。即便沃什

能够说服其他委员同事放弃抬高利率，从过去两次 FOMC 会议后的市场表现来看，市场会通过远端利率进一
步的冲高来预防通胀脱锚的风险。

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) STRESS TEST삼성전자(005930) STRESS TESTSTRESS TEST - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

반도체(Overweight): Stress Test  
78 
삼성전자  (005930) 
2026.11.04

STRESS TEST 
[반도체/전기전자] 고의영 
2122-9179 ey.ko@imfnsec.com

목표주가 37만원, ‘28년 기준으로는 56만원까지 열림 
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 37만원으로 커버리지를 개시한다. ‘27년 BPS에 목표 
P/B 2.3배를 적용했다. 이는 ‘00년 이후 25년간 평균 P/B의 +2표준편차에 
해당한다[표1]. ‘26~’28년 ROE는 각각 56%, 59%, 48%로 지난 25년 평균인 
22%를 크게 상회할 전망이며, 고원형 ROE가 장기간 유지될 것으로 판단한다.  
이익 지속성에 대한 믿음이 공고해질 경우 시장의 시야는 ‘28년으로 향할 것이다. 
이 경우 ‘28년 BPS 기준 목표주가는 56만원까지 열릴 수 있다[표2]. 
동사 주가는 ‘26년 6월 고점 대비 10월 저점 기준 43% 하락해 과거 다운사이클 
평균 하락폭인 -41%를 넘어섰다. 특히 과거 8~13개월에 걸쳐 진행됐던 조정이 
이번에는 4개월 만에 나타났다. 시장이 ‘28년 이익 급감을 상정하고 있는 것이다. 
이러한 우려가 지속되는 한, 이익의 눈높이가 높아질수록 멀티플은 저렴해질 
뿐이며, DRAM 가격이 예상보다 더 올라갈 것이라는 소식이 주가의 촉매로 
작용하지 못한다. 결국 주가 반등을 위해서는 시장이 상정하고 있는 다운사이클이 
우려만큼 깊지 않을 것이라는 믿음이 필요한 것으로 본다.

STRESS TEST와 HBM 재평가 
① 당사는 고객들이 어느 정도 가격대에서 LTA를 해지하려는 유인이 생길지 
추정해봤다. Micron의 코멘트를 차용하여 ‘22~’23년 수준의 스트레스 테스트를 
시나리오 분석한 결과, 4Q27 고점 대비 DRAM 가격이 60% 수준 빠져야 해지가 
검토될 수 있다[그림2]. 그마저도 다음 공급 부족기의 물량 배정 우선순위 상실 
등 정성적 요인까지 감안하면 실제 해지 가능성은 더욱 낮은 것으로 판단한다. 
② HBM 비중이 높아질수록 동사의 이익 변동성은 낮아질 것이다. 특히 
중장기적으로 확대될 cHBM은 고객별 수주생산 제품으로 현물시장과 유통 
재고가 없고, 생산에 앞서 물량과 가격이 결정된다. 수요가 둔화되더라도 범용 
메모리처럼 재고 누적과 가격 급락으로 이어지기보다는 발주 조정이나 계약 
재협상을 통해 영향을 받는 구조다.  
동사 HBM 매출은 ‘26년 28조원에서 ‘28년 215조원으로 증가할 전망이다. 
‘27년 HBM 가격 방향성의 윤곽이 잡힌 현 시점에서, 동사 HBM 매출의 변수는 
가격이 아니라 물량이며, 물량은 고객과 사전에 협의되는 구조다. 과거 메모리 
업황에서는 2년 뒤의 이익을 가늠하기 어려웠으나, 이제는 HBM을 통해 중장기 
실적을 논의할 수 있게 됐다는 점, 그 자체에 주목한다. 
만약 동사의 ‘26~’28년 HBM 영업이익에 시기별 TSMC P/E (21배, 15배, 12배)를 
적용하면 HBM 가치는 동사 시가총액의 19%, 69%, 108%를 설명한다[표3]. 
특히 cHBM이 본격화할 ‘28년에는 HBM만으로 현재 시가총액이 설명될 수 
있다는 의미다. 시장이 통상 받아들이는 10배를 타겟팅해도 결과는 크게 다르지 
않다.

반도체(Overweight): Stress Test  
79

‘27년 주가 경로: 회복, 박스권, 재평가 
연말까지는 급락 이후 저점을 높여가는 회복 구간일 전망이다.  
‘27년 상반기까지는 ‘28년 업황 지속성에 대한 우려가 상존할 전망이다. 이 불확실성이 
멀티플을 누르는 동안 ‘27년 이익은 계속 상향되므로 EPS 상승과 멀티플 하락이 상쇄되는 
구간이 이어질 전망이다. 기회비용은 결국 환원수익률로 보상받아야 하므로, ‘27년 1월 신규 
주주환원 정책이 중요한 변곡점이 될 것으로 판단한다.  
당사는 동사가 ‘27~’29년 FCF의 50%를 주주에게 환원하고, 이 중 70%를 배당, 30%를 
자사주 매입/소각으로 쓰는 것으로 가정하여, 현재 주가 기준 ‘27년 15% 수준의 
주주환원수익률을 기대한다. 미국 10년물 국채 금리가 5% 안팎인 환경에서 무위험 금리의 
3배에 달하는 환원수익률, 그중 배당만으로 10% 수준의 수익률은 그 자체로 희소한 투자 
포인트로 볼 수 있다. 
‘27년 하반기 이후에는, 실적 추정의 기준 연도가 ‘28년으로 넘어가고, 이후 신규 팹 물량이 
소량 출하되면서 센티먼트상 우려는 커질 것이다. 그러나 LTA가 이익을 지킨다는 사실이 
숫자로 확인되고 cHBM의 가치가 재평가된다면 순환 할인이 축소될 것으로 판단한다.

업사이드 요인 
① AI 투자 지속성 우려로 최근 주가 하락이 가팔랐던 만큼, 이 부분에 대한 우려가 
완화되면 반등의 속도와 폭이 예상보다 빠를 수 있다 
② 향후 주주환원 규모가 예상보다 더 적극적일 경우 추가적인 업사이드가 될 수 있다. 
혹은 주주환원 이후에도 남는 대규모 FCF의 활용 방안도 촉매가 될 수 있다.

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상 
산업추천 투자등급  
시가총액기준 산업별 시장비중대비 보유비중의 변화를 추천하는 것임 
 Overweight(비중확대) 
 Neutral(중립) 
 Underweight(비중축소)

반도체업종

◼ 3분기 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 
◼ 주주환원은 시점의 문제일 뿐. 강화 방향성은 여전히 유효. 
◼ 메모리반도체 이익의 폭증이 가격의 매력을 유발. 비중확대 전략 유효.

매력적인 가격, 변하지 않은 주주환원 정책의 방향성 
목표주가와 매수의견 유지. 메모리반도체 주도 폭발적 이익 상승이 가격 (주
가)의 매력을 지속 유발하고 있고, 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향성에도 변한 것
은 없다는 판단. 절대적으로 싼 주가를 비중 확대의 기회로 삼을 필요. 
1. 주주환원, 시점의 문제 
금번 실적발표에 대한 시장의 반응은 다소 냉소적. 대외 불확실성이 큰 만큼, 
시장은 자사주 매입/소각과 같은 즉각적 주가 부양 정책의 발표를 기대.  
당사는 삼성전자의 주주환원이 “시점의 문제”라 생각. 총 주주환원의 규모도, 
차기 (2027-2029년) 주주환원 정책의 강화 방향에도 변한 것은 없다는 판
단. 시장이 기대하는 자사주 매입/소각은 우선주 중심으로 시행될 가능성이 
높으며, 시행 시, 그 규모는 25조원 내외일 것 (DPS 12,567원)으로 전망. 
2. 메모리반도체, 더 올라갈 것 
보수적 환율 전망 (2027년 평균 환율 1,300원 적용)에도, 2027년 메모리반도
체 영업이익은 608조원에 달할 것. 이익 전망의 상향 여력 또한 유효. 
1) 최대 생산, 최대 판매 
삼성전자는 금일 4Q26 DRAM 판매량 가이던스로 전분기 대비 10% 중후반 
성장 (q-q +18%로 추정)을 제시. 일부 투자자들은 높아진 기저 부담으로 
2027년 판매 성장이 제한될 것을 우려. 당사는 높아진 기저에도 2027년 
DRAM 판매가 전년대비 20% 상승할 것으로 전망. 
- 극단적 수급 불균형 속, 삼성전자는 “최대 생산, 최대 판매” 정책을 시행 
중. 삼성전자만의 강력한 Execution이 20% 대 성장을 유지시킬 원동력. 
- DRAM 1c 수율의 조기 개선 속, 범용 DRAM 양산에 DRAM 1c Capa가 일
부 투입될 것으로 예상. Bit 성장을 높일 수 있는 변화가 될 것. 
2) 열려 있는 가격의 상방 
1Q27 모바일 DRAM에서 Surprise가 발생할 것이라는 의견을 유지 (전분기 
대비 30% 이상 상승). 장기계약이 체결된 CSP향으로도 가격의 추가 상승 여
력이 존재한다는 점을 상기할 필요. CSP의 경우, 당장의 비용 부담보다 AI 시
장 선점을 우선시. 장기계약으로 형성된 가격 Band의 천장 (Ceiling) 또한 시
장 우려 대비 낮지 않음을 감안할 필요. 2027년 범용 DRAM ASP는 전년대비 
40% 중후반까지 상승할 가능성 (현 전망: 전년대비 34% 상승).  
3) HBM, 제품 경쟁력과 점유율의 동반 성장  
최근 삼성전자향으로 HBM4/HBM4e 추가 공급 요청이 쇄도하고 있는 것으
로 추정. 일부 고객은 연내 추가 공급 시 2027년 계약 가격을 선제 지급할 
의사를 표출. 제품 경쟁력이 회복되었음을 보여주는 증거라 생각. 
제품 경쟁력의 회복과 최대 Capa의 이점 속, 2027년은 HBM 점유율 1위를 
확보하는 기념비적 해가 될 것으로 전망 (매출액 기준 점유율 38% 예상).

### Page 1

2026.10.30   
한화에어로스페이스  
(012450)

3Q26: 그래도 믿는다  Company Brief

3분기 매출 6조 943억원/영업이익 4,520억원 
한화에어로스페이스의 3분기 매출은 6조 943억원/영업이익 4,520억원(OPM 7.4%)로 
컨센서스와 당사 추정치를 모두 대폭 하회하는 실적을 기록하였다. 앞서 실적을 발표한 
한화오션의 실적 쇼크를 감안하더라도, 주력사업인 지상방산 부문의 실적이 매출 1조 
2,455억원/영업이익 217억원(1.7%)로 지난 분기(2조 1,075억원/ 5,330억원/ 25.3%) 
는 물론 전년동기(2조 1,098억원/5,725억원/27.1%)보다도 현저히 감소하였다. 
일회성 요인은 대전사업장 폭발사고에 따른 복구비용 등 300억원, 일부 국내사업 
충당금 160억원 및 환율하락에 따른 효과 등이 있었으나 역시 이를 감안해도 내수와 
수출 모두 실질 이익률은 5%미만으로 추정된다. 지상방산 분기 매출은 1분기(1조 
2,211억)보다 오히려 소폭 증가하였으나 영업이익이 1분기 2,087억원에서 
217억원으로 대폭 감소한 점에 대하여 회사는 프로젝트별 믹스 구성에 따른 낮은 
이익률의 인도분이 반영되었다고 설명하고 있으나, 그럼에도 불구하고 수출과 내수 
이익률 모두 BEP에 가까웠다는 점은 쉽게 이해가 가지는 않는다. 다만 방산기업의 
특성상 대전사업장 사고에 따른 실제 납기 영향 등 회사가 공개할 수 없는 사정이 
있었을 것으로 추측할 뿐이다. 대전사업장은 현재 복구작업이 진행 중이며 27년 
상반기 중에는 가동 정상화할 것으로 예상된다.  
긍정적인 소식도 있다. 폴란드향 천무의 연간 인도대수가 기존 가이던스인 40대에서 
60대로 증가하였으며, 이를 포함하여 3분기에 인도되지 않은 물량의 대부분이 
4분기에 집중될 예정으로, 올해의 4분기 실적 개선폭은 통상적으로 볼 수 있는 
4분기의 계절적 집중도보다 더 커질 것으로 예상한다. 방산기업의 실적은 인도일정에 
따라 크게 변할 수 있는 분기 실적보다 연간 합산이 더 중요하다는 점을 한 번 더 
믿고, 4분기를 기다리는 게 좋겠다.

투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 1,200,000원 하향  
투자의견 BUY를 유지하지만, SOTP기반 목표주가는 기존 1,490,000원에서 
1,200,000원으로 19.5% 하향한다. 비영업가치 중 자회사 한화시스템과 한화오션, 
쎄트렉아이의 지분가치 하락(합산 8.7조원→7.1조원), 그리고 지난 분기 대비 늘어난 
별도 순차입금(3.25조→4.95조원)을 반영하였으며 최근 방산섹터 전반의 밸류 하락을 
감안하여 영업가치 중 지상방산+항공우주의 Target P/E를 기존 35배에서 30배로 
조정하였다. 
3분기는 여러모로 악재가 겹쳤다. 주식시장 전반의 하락에 더해 방산섹터 수주 지연에 
따른 디레이팅, 그리고 납기 지연에 따른 실적 하락 등 나올 수 있는 악재는 다 
나왔다고도 볼 수 있다. 그러나 뒤집어보면 지연중인 수주는 시간의 문제일 뿐 가시화 
가능성이 매우 높으며, 실적 또한 진행률 인식이 아닌 인도기준 인식에 따른 일시적 
현상으로 4분기 이후 복구될 가능성이 크다. 펀더멘털의 유의미한 변화는 없다고 볼 
수 있으며 밸류에이션 매력도가 증가한 현 시점에서 추가 매수를 고려할 때다. 다만, 
대전사업장 사고에 따른 당국의 제재조치가 아직 확정되지 않았음은 잔여 리스크 
요인이다. 동종사의 신규계약 입찰제한 제재조치가 주가에 상당한 영향을 미쳤음을 
고려하면 한화에어로스페이스 또한 제재조치 여부를 지켜본 후 투자할 것을 권한다.

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
 당 연구원은 한화에어로스페이스(012450)의 일부 비용을 지원받아 한화에어로스페이스 공장 투어(2026년 10월 1-2)를 다녀왔습니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

Company Brief 
유틸리티/건설 
Analyst 문경원, CFA 
kyeongwon.moon@meritz.co.kr 
RA 이동석 
dongseok.lee@meritz.co.kr 3Q26 Review: 바닥을 잡는다면 지금  
믹스 개선에 따른 이익률 개선 조금씩 관찰 중 
3Q26 연결 영업이익은 2,612억원(+90.6% YoY)을 기록하며 컨센서스(2,455억원)
를 소폭 상회했다. 밥캣(1,674억원)이 컨센서스(1,761억원)를 소폭 하회한 가운데, 
에너빌리티 부문 영업이익(+179% YoY, OPM 6.6%)이 호실적을 이끌었다. 환율 하
락에도 불구, 가스터빈, 원전 등 고수익성 프로젝트의 비중 상승이 주요 원인이다. 
3Q26 누적 수주는 10.0조원(+85.5% YoY)을 기록했다. 4Q26 가스복합 EPC 수주 
등을 감안하면 연간 가이던스(13.3조원) 달성은 무난하다. 한수원과의 협상 결렬로 
이목이 집중된 체코 두코바니 시공 수주는 2027년 인식될 확률이 높아 보인다. 주간
사가 바뀌더라도 이미 프로젝트에 참여 중인 동사를 배제하기는 쉽지 않다.

대미투자 이후, 연말~연초 수주 모멘텀에 집중 
연말 연초에 다음과 수주 모멘텀이 집중되면서, 주가 상승을 이끌 전망이다. 
1) 대형원전: 정부 자금 중심의 Westinghouse LLI(장납기 기자재) 발주가 몰린다. 
우선 미국 DOE(에너지부)가 제공한 대출을 기반으로 미국 수주가 기대된다.  폴란드 
정부 역시 최근 LLI 발주 작업을 시작했다. 한편, 한국 정부는 연말까지 100억 달러 
규모의 대미 투자 송금을 협의 중인데, 이 역시 DOC(상무부) 주도의 대형 원전 건설
을 위한 LLI 발주에 쓰일 가능성이 높다 
2) SMR: 오래 기다려왔던 Nuscale 향 수주가 시작되고 있다. 우선 29일 모듈 1기 
제작 계약을 체결했다. 현금이 부족한 Nuscale이 용기를 냈다는 건, 곧 Nuscale-
TVA 간 PPA 가 임박했음을 의미한다. 본격적인 수주는 PPA 직후 예상된다.  
3) 가스 터빈: 2026년 초 해외 수주(7기)는 2029년까지 인도 계획이다. 이후 2030
년 슬랏 예약을 완료하고 2031년 물량을 논의 중으로 보인다. 지금은 2030년 슬랏
에 대한 PO가 나와 수주잔고에 인식할 타이밍이다. 역시 수출 물량으로 추측한다.

성장에 대한 기대가 사라진 밸류에이션, 지금이 바닥 
투자의견 Buy를 유지, 할인율 상승을 반영하여 적정주가를 109,000원으로 -17% 
하향 조정한다. 금리 상승, 환율 하락, 트럼프 지지율 하락 등 Top-down 리스크에 
최근 조정을 받았지만, 추가적인 주가 하락 폭은 제한적일 전망이다. EV/선행 수주잔
고 지표는 현재 1.0배 수준까지 하락하며, 원전의 성장성이 주목받지 못했던 
2021~2024년 수준에 근접하게 내려왔다.  
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 30

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

Document 260: AI“大到不能倒”了？特朗普亲自营救黄仁勋，被指是“现代水门事件”！

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI“大到不能倒”了？特朗普亲自营救黄仁勋，被指是“现代水门事件”！.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-24
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

AI 产业已深度嵌⼊美国经济，甚⾄到了 “ ⼤⽽不能倒 ” 的地步。 AI 抛售引发衰退恐慌，促使特
朗普政府施压英伟达董事会、⼒挺⻩仁勋复职，推动英伟达股价创纪录暴涨。但这场救市
⾏动正引发国会调查，被指具有 “ ⽔⻔事件 ” 特征，政治⻛险与 AI 估值隐患仍未消退。
摘要：
特朗普政府对英伟达董事会施压、推动⻩仁勋复职⼀事，正从⼀场市场救援⾏动演变为潜在的
政治危机。
据 Fox Business 记者 Charles Gasparino 援引知情⼈⼠消息，促使财政部⻓⻉森特转变⽴场、⼒
挺⻩仁勋复职的核⼼动因，是对经济衰退的恐惧 ——AI 股票的抛售潮令主要投资者向⽩宫周边
⼈⼠传递警告，称美国陷⼊衰退的概率 " 接近 100%" 。⻩仁勋复职消息公布后，英伟达单⽇暴涨
逾 34% ，创 2000 年以来最⼤单⽇涨幅，市值单⽇增加逾 1.1 万亿美元。
然⽽，Gasparino 将特朗普的这⼀操作称为 " 现代版⽔⻔事件 " 。1972 年美国总统⼤选期间，尼
克松竞选团队成员潜⼊⺠主党总部安装窃听器并被捕。此后，⽩宫掩盖真相、阻挠调查的⾏为

曝光，最终迫使尼克松于 1974 年辞职，成为美国历史上⾸位辞职的总统。
Gasparino 警告称国会调查已在路上，⺠主党⼈正将 " ⻩仁勋事件 " 纳⼊弹劾特朗普的议程，短期
市场提振背后潜伏着巨⼤的政治与市场⻛险。
救市逻辑：衰退警告促使⽩宫转向
据 Gasparino 报道，在上周 AI 股票遭到重挫后，主要投资者向⽩宫周边⼈⼠传递警告，认为 AI 交
易的平仓将冲击 " 整个科技⽣态系统 " 的⽀出，经济衰退概率 " 接近 100%" 。这⼀警告被视为⻉森
特急转弯的 " 主要原因 "—— 就在⼋天前，⻉森特还曾要求英伟达董事会在公司外部寻找⻩仁勋的
继任者，⽽到了周⽇，他已将所谓的间谍⻛险斥为 " 某种程度上的⽆中⽣有 " 。
Nomura 分析师 Charlie McElligott 在周⼀给客⼾的报告中指出，AI 与股市已 " ⼤到不能倒 "。他描
述了复职消息触发的机械式市场反应： " 对冲头⼨被强制平仓，空头遭到⽌损，标普期货被拉
爆，市场疯狂追涨。 "
穆迪⾸席经济学家 Mark Zandi 从宏观视⻆提供了另⼀维度的注脚。他估计，今年美国实际 GDP

增⻓中，最多有三分之⼀可归因于 AI 产业繁荣，并表⽰：
Coherence Credit Strategies 的 Sal Naro 向彭博表⽰，这代表了华尔街的共识判断：
市场反弹：机械买盘有限，真实买家仍待⼊场
英伟达单⽇涨幅超 34% 为市场提供了强⼼剂，但分析⼈⼠警告，技术性反弹⼒度有其天花板。
"AI 正在驱动整个经济列⻋。如果 AI 脱轨，整个经济也将随之受到冲击。 "
" 全球 AI 繁荣已经⼤到不容失败。 "
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

据 Nomura 模型测算，杠杆 ETF 与趋势跟踪基⾦合计形成约 600 亿美元的被动强制买盘。但⼀旦
标普 500 指数突破 7043 点，商品交易顾问（ CTA ）持仓将回归平仓状态，在指数触及 7625 点之
前不再有新增买盘压⼒。此后，市场能否持续上涨，将完全依赖真实资⾦的主动介⼊。
Goldman Sachs 分析师 Anton Tran 在其流动性报告中措辞克制： " 我还不确定市场的命运⼏乎等
同于⼀个⼈的命运，究竟是好事还是坏事。不过，我们并不挑剔。 "
Evercore ISI 的 TMT 交易团队态度则更为明确，其周⼀报告认为：
政治⻛险：国会调查逼近， " ⽔⻔ " 阴影挥之不去
Gasparino 的担忧并不⽌于市场层⾯。他将特朗普此番操作定性为具有 " ⽔⻔事件 " 特征 —— 通过
向董事会施压、在政治问题上 " ⼤做⽂章 " ，为⻩仁勋复职铺路，⽽这⼀路径本⾝已引发反弹。
⻩仁勋将于 12 ⽉ 1 ⽇接受三个众议院委员会的闭⻔录⾳听证，英伟达总法律顾问 Tim Teter 随后于

12 ⽉ 2 ⽇出席。⺠主党⼈要求调阅⻩仁勋与特朗普之间通话的所有记录，⽽特朗普本⼈在公开场
合炫耀⾃⼰已说服董事会，实际上已将这些通话置于调查的核⼼。
Semafor 主编 Ben Smith 认同 Gasparino 对华盛顿政治⾛向的判断；保守派评论⼈ Erick Erickson
亦表⽰，此事 " 将成为⼀个⼤问题，尤其是如果⻩仁勋或 Lutnick 的⼦⼥存在商业利益关联 " 。
英伟达董事会本⾝也未将此事视为已了结：董事会已成⽴委员会，正在聘任⾸席合规官，并表
⽰对该事件的内部调查将持续⾄结案。
⻓期隐患：⻩仁勋回归能否真正⽌住跌势？
⽆论政治如何演变，市场观察⼈⼠对⻩仁勋复职能否从根本上扭转 AI 估值修正存有保留。
Gasparino 本⼈对这⼀救市逻辑持审慎态度，他称此举是 " ⼀次⼤胆的策略，或许在强⼤合⼒的
作⽤下奏效 " ，但同时警告 " ⻛险正在快速积累 " ：
市场策略师 Gary Kaltbaum 对衰退警告的回应更加直⽩：
Gasparino 还转述了市场流传的两种解读：⼀是特朗普此举为记者奉上了 " ⽔⻔事件以来最好的

新闻素材 " ；⼆是⻩仁勋复职不过是在 "AI 交易⻓期已废 " 的背景下，为持仓股东提供了⼀个退出
流动性窗⼝。对此， Gasparino 表⽰： " 我认为两者皆有。 "
以美国家庭持有的股票资产接近 GDP 的 200% 这⼀背景衡量，市场能否在技术性反弹耗尽之后获
得真实资⾦的接⼒，或许才是这场政治与市场博弈最终成败的真正试⾦⽯。
" 当 AI 基础设施建设的历史被书写时，今天理应拥有独⽴的⼀章。这实际上是 AI 产业的第⼀次救
助⾏动 —— 救助的不是某家公司的资产负债表，⽽是⽀撑着⼀场万亿美元级产业建设的底层架
构。"
" ⻩仁勋⽆法再像过去那样做⽣意。国会调查已在路上。 "
" ⽆论如何，该收缩的还是会收缩。 "

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -32.53 điểm tương ứng -1.83%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Linh kiện ô tô, Dầu khí và Công nghệ là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -634 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, ACB và VPL; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-609 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -5,829 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -1,462 hợp đồng. Tự doanh duy trì mua ròng 722 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -5,278 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam đánh mất đà hồi phục của tuần trước, khi VN-Index quay đầu giảm khoảng 
1,8% về đóng cửa quanh 1.742 điểm, với phiên cuối tuần có thời điểm lùi sâu về sát 1.727 
điểm trước khi thu hẹp đà giảm. Dù mùa báo cáo KQKD quý 3 diễn ra tương đối tích cực, 
tuy nhiên các lo ngại về việc môi trường lãi suất cao vẫn duy trì trong bối cảnh căng thẳng 
Trung Đông leo thang đang đè nặng lên tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Kết quả kỳ họp của FED trong 
tuần qua nhìn chung phù hợp với kỳ vọng và không gây ra ảnh hưởng đáng kể. Điểm kém 
tích cực nhất là khối ngoại đã quay lại bán ròng với tổng giá trị khoảng 2.700 tỷ đồng 
trong tuần — đảo ngược diễn biến mua ròng cuối tuần trước, cho thấy đà rút vốn ngoại 
vẫn chưa thực sự dừng lại.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tuần qua hình thành mẫu nến giảm trên đồ thị tuần, nhưng chưa thoái lui quá 1 
nửa cây nến tăng của tuần trước, cùng thanh khoản được tiết giảm, hàm ý nhịp điều chỉnh 
vẫn trong phạm vi cho phép. Trên khung ngày, sau bốn phiên giằng co đầu tuần với thanh 
khoản thấp, chỉ số đã chịu áp lực sụt giảm mạnh hơn trong phiên cuối tuần, chớm xuống 
dưới đường tín hiệu chính giữa dải Bollinger, nhưng cho tín hiệu rút chân ngay sau đó, thể 
hiện áp lực bán tháo đã tạm thời được kiểm soát. Mặc dù rủi ro mở rộng thêm nhịp điều 
chỉnh vẫn còn để ngỏ, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được bảo lưu, VNINDEX 
được kỳ vọng sẽ cho phản ứng hồi phục trở lại tại các điểm đỡ bên dưới.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên)

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 November 23, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
AI Implies Dramatic Shift in the Structure of the U.S. Economy
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
On November 16, Federal Reserve Chairman Kevin Warsh moderated three conference
sessions at a symposium focused on how AI will ripple through the economy. The event
was the first public look at one of the five task forces Warsh created to review the
Federal Reserve (Fed) processes for doing its job. The task forces are meant to help

reform the Fed by building a consensus for overhaul to address shortcomings Mr. Warsh
has raised about the Fed's performance over the years. The outsized role of AI in the
evolution of the U.S. economy is reflected in the task force to consider AI's impact on
employment and productivity. This is, perhaps, the least controversial of the various
issues the task forces are charged with considering but nevertheless one of major
importance.
The main takeaway from the symposium was that various scenarios remain possible

with quite different implications for the future of U.S. growth, employment and interest
rates. The wide range of outcomes has contributed to the recent volatility in equity
markets, especially the parts of the market most closely associated with the buildout
of AI data centers. Estimating the payoff from the buildout naturally remains a key
focus for investors but one that is subject to uncertainty because of the rapid pace of
technological innovation and the political pressures that are arising from public concerns

over the changes AI is bringing.
Exhibit 1 helps shed light on the dramatic consequences AI is likely to create for the
structure of the U.S. economy and employment. The cost structure of corporate output
as of 2025 includes about half of each dollar for labor. An estimate for the cost structure
of a dollar spent on inferential AI after the data center buildout reaches a net stock
centered around $5 trillion in 2030 requires only a fourth as much labor per unit of

revenue or output. The bigger the share of overall value inferential AI becomes in gross
domestic product (GDP) the lower the labor share of income will become. This is not a
new trend. The labor share of income has been gradually declining ever since the digital
economy began to develop over a half century ago. What is new is the speed of the
transformation. Self-generative AI is representative of the singularity, the point when the
spread of this digital transformation becomes exponential.

The drastically lower labor share of value implied by AI output implies that other costs
comprise bigger shares. For example, the exhibit shows the profit share of revenue is
much higher (28% vs.19%) for AI-generated output. This is already evident in the profit
profile of the S&P 500 where the margins and profit growth of the AI-related companies
are orders of magnitude greater than the profit profiles of non-AI-related businesses.
Presumably, as the old economy increasingly transforms to this new economy cost

structure, the productivity benefits of AI will increasingly transfer over to traditional
businesses and transform their cost structures in the direction of those of inferential AI
builders. This analysis suggests profit growth is moving toward a bigger share of output
over the next few years.
Competition naturally eats away at "excess" profits, those not justified by costs. Exhibit 1
partly illustrates the justification for higher AI profits. AI is capital-intensive compared

to the non-AI economy. This can be seen in the much higher depreciation-cost share
in the AI economy (28% vs.15%). AI capital is more short-lived than average, reflecting
the quicker obsolescence that naturally results from accelerating technological change
and less durable infrastructure (short-lived chips). This issue of rapid depreciation has
surfaced in the debate over potential AI profitability, with analysts trying to figure the
right life span of data centers to model their profit outlook.

Investment Implications
AI's creative destruction is likely to
leave a lot of laggards in its wake while
creating a stronger, more profitable
economy for investors. As a result,
we believe a diversified investment
approach favoring high-quality balance
sheets and reliable free cash flows is
warranted.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 23, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Finally, "other costs" make up about a third of AI output cost compared to just 12% for
non-AI output. Among these are the increased power costs associated with intensive
compute processing. Electricity costs are going to replace a lot of labor costs. As the
midterms made clear, rules and regulation for data centers, including power and water
issues, are likely to come under much greater scrutiny as well as other environmental and

"quality-of-life" issues.
Ultimately, this new cost structure for the AI economy will be the focus of innovation
to bring down costs and remove bottlenecks. Technology is ultimately deflationary.
Monetary policy is the cause of inflation. This "deflationary" or declining cost aspect
of technological progress is a key factor behind the notion that the promise of the
singularity is a world of superabundance. Intelligence, power and compute costs are likely

to become cheaper and cheaper as innovators work to create this superabundance.
Exhibit 1: Comparison of Corporate Cost Structure to Produce 2025 Output to
Corporate Cost Structure to Produce Inference Compute Revenue from AI Data
Center Buildout by 2030.

Wages Profit Depreciation Other
Source: "The AI Capex Boom--Rewiring the U.S. Economy," Applied Global Macro Research, November 5, 2026.

Employment and AI. The dramatic reduction in labor needed to run an AI cost structure
promises a much higher level of productivity and therefore higher levels of wealth and
income. As the cost structure in Exhibit 1 illustrates, however, a much bigger share
of output goes to capital and therefore shareholders in an AI-dominated economic
structure. There are various ways this change could sort itself out. In a society with an
increasingly older population, more capital income solves the problem of more retirees

needing to live on investment income. AI also promises to increase longevity. Therefore,
the future is likely to include more elderly people living on capital income as well as
more elderly people working. In addition, the bounty from higher productivity is likely
to support demand for new goods and services as past technology breakthroughs have
shown. People will be required to produce those new goods and services.
Nevertheless, it's clear from the distribution of capital income that's becoming more, not

less, concentrated, that policies that spread wealth ownership across a broader swath
of the population could potentially help ease the social tensions plaguing the population
under the guise of "affordability." The proliferation of gifts from the wealthy to Trump
Accounts is a reflection of the recognition that this issue needs to be addressed. The
U.S. Treasury rolled out temporary regulations at the end of September that allow Trump
Accounts to hold donated individual stocks instead of just diversified index funds. There

is likely to be a push to expand the growth of large philanthropic donations from the
private sector to fund retirement-type accounts for the broader population. This is an
alternative to higher tax rates that fund government spending rather than long-term
saving. A society that shares more broadly in the fruits of AI is more likely to tolerate AI's
profound changes.

### Page 4

4 of 8 November 23, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Too Connected to Fail: When the Jetsons Meet the Flintstones
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
This month, when a leading U.S. technology company went dark, planes across America
were grounded, and major banks went offline, investors learned an unsettling lesson on
the fragility of America’s digital economy.  We learned that interconnectivity is both a

strength and a weakness—and that the digital economy of the future rests upon the
shaky infrastructure of the past.
We live in a world where AI is advancing at warp speed. Computing power is expanding
exponentially. Financial transactions move around the globe in milliseconds. And cloud
computing keeps billions of people and businesses connected every day. It is a world
where everything is faster, digital and connected.
The U.S. digital economy now accounts for roughly 12% of GDP, expanding about three

times faster than the economy overall this century. Digital gross output totaled $3.7
trillion in 2021, the last year of available data from the Commerce Department; that said,
the figure almost certainly understates the current size of the U.S. digital economy. Digital
commerce now accounts for nearly 18% of all retail sales ($350 billion), although again,
this number is understated. Add in business-to-business digital transactions and digitally

delivered professional services, and the amount of daily digital commerce in the U.S. is
massive and growing robustly. Each one of these transactions ultimately depends on
communications services, electricity, computing facilities, and digital payments networks.
Yet here’s the rub: our digital world is juxtaposed against a physical infrastructure
of aging radar systems, antiquated telecommunications equipment, half-century-old
transmission lines, vulnerable underwater subsea cables and decades-old software.

Here we are, well into the 21st century, and a handful of Federal Aviation Administration
facilities are still using floppy disks to support aviation travel. Our world is a world where
the space-age Jetsons have collided with the Stone Age Flintstones.
Something had to give, and it did this November: business communications were
disrupted, planes were grounded, ATMs were frozen and investors were left wondering
“what’s next”?
In our opinion, “what’s next” entails a renewed focus and commitment on the part of the

U.S. public and private sectors to build America’s physical infrastructure back better and
faster. The recent disruptions, we believe, are a reminder that a nation’s infrastructure is
essentially its backbone, and every nation, like every human being, is only as strong as its
skeletal framework.
A healthy backbone allows an economy to move, pivot, grow, innovate and compete. An
impaired spine, in contrast, constrains mobility, undermines nimbleness and flexibility and

is prone to setbacks.
So, think of roads, bridges, ports, airports, electrical grids, communication networks and,
increasingly, digital networks not merely as conveniences of modern life. Rather, think
of them as foundational components of economic activity, national security and global
competitiveness. Without them, even the world’s most sophisticated economy can be
brought to a standstill.
The great irony—and potential opportunity—of the “too connected to fail” is that while the

U.S. leads the world in AI, cloud computing, and other advanced technological capabilities,
the physical infrastructure of the nation continues to show its age. Electrical grids are
struggling to accommodate increasing demand. Transmission networks are plagued with
bottlenecks. Aviation communications systems built in the 1970s are overwhelmed.
None of the above is new—America’s infrastructure deficiencies have been widely
acknowledged but frequently tolerated for decades. The grid held up most of the time,

while airline safety remained relatively stellar. Power outages during storms and heat
waves were expected and tolerated—no matter the misery created. All good.
Investment Implications
The future is digital, which
requires more capital investment
on America’s aging physical
infrastructure. We continue to favor
Industrials, infrastructure-related
firms, and commodities for the long-
run.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 23, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Today, however, it’s different. Because there is so much riding on our digital
connectivity—think trillions of dollars of daily commerce—the cost of failure or
disruption continues to soar. Case in point: America’s recent aviation meltdown. The
communications malfunction triggered a national four-hour ground stop, resulting in
nearly 20,000 flight cancellations over four days, at a potential cost to the airlines

of more than $1 billion in profits. Adding more salt to the wound, fried and frizzled
passengers absorbed an estimated $1.9 billion in additional economic costs. All because
the communications infrastructure supporting the world’s most sophisticated aviation
system failed to handle a technical upgrade.
Where are the Jetsons when you need them? We believe the recent technological failures
will accelerate infrastructure spending for a number of reasons. One, as shown above,

the economic costs of digital failures are rising. Two, America’s digital economy is now
too big—and too connected—to fail. Three, the extraordinary power needed to run
the AI revolution makes it absolutely essential that the U.S. stop talking about fixing
its aging infrastructure and start doing it. Four, with the stakes so large for America’s
largest technology companies, with enormous incentives of their own, these firms are
contributing their private capital of their own to build back America’s infrastructure

faster and better. And fifth, an aging air traffic control system, an unreliable national
grid, and weathered transmission lines―these are more than just economic and financial
challenges. They are challenges to America’s national security—notably to the great
power rivalry between the U.S. and China. This rivalry, in our opinion, will help create
more bipartisan support for increased infrastructure spending and greater public-private
sector cooperation.

Investment summary. It’s taken a series of technological snafus, but here we are—
America’s infrastructure is gaining the attention—and capital—it needs. It’s also
garnered the attention of Congress. Just last week, the Bipartisan American Affordability
Act, which reduces permitting burdens on a number of projects, passed a key procedural
vote in the Senate. Whether the House votes in favor of the bill remains to be seen but
in general, the U.S.’s cumbersome permitting and regulatory systems are coming under

more scrutiny.
To this point, the bipartisan National Governors Association recently outlined
recommendations designed to cut through the permitting and regulatory red tape. The
Department of Energy has developed a coordinated federal transmission permitting
framework designed to clear and approve projects at a faster pace. Yes, it will take
some time to cut through the labyrinth of federal, state and local approvals, permits, and
regulations. But the need for reforms has become quite evident and urgent, with political

inaction less acceptable to an electorate increasingly worried and wary of the next
technology glitch.
So, who potentially benefits in our theme of “too connected to fail”?  In our opinion,
the winners will include electrical equipment manufacturers, engineering firms, utilities,
telecommunications infrastructure providers, industrial automation companies, and
specialized construction firms. Specialists in maritime surveillance, underwater sonar
detection, and fiber-optics sensing capabilities are well positioned in this new world.

In terms of asset allocation, this means considering more exposure to infrastructure-
related industrial companies and leaders in renewables (solar, wind, electric vehicles,
biomass) and the required infrastructure behind each renewable energy source. Leaders
in electricity distribution, charging stations, batteries, as well as low-carbon hydrogen,
biomethane, and advanced biofuels should also be considered in portfolios. Ditto for
leaders in low-carbon technologies (LED lighting, smart energy meters, and storage),

and leaders in transmission technologies like high-voltage direct current (HVDC), which
transfers wind and solar power from where it is created to where it is needed.  We also
still favor commodities over the long term, as well as cybersecurity leaders.

### Page 6

6 of 8 November 23, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
In our view, what does the market want and need to see to establish a firmer base?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
IInvestors are looking to reprice uncertainty for 2027. A lot has been digested by this
market since semiconductor stocks peaked in June. Inflation, Fed policy, geopolitics,
the funding of the AI buildout, CEO changes and future power adjustments out of the

midterm elections all in a matter of months.
Most importantly, investors kept clinging to the base—earnings and profitability. As long
as the base remained strong and growing, investors were willing to take the risk of a
market led by technology and the AI buildout more specifically.
Recently, investors have shifted to wanting more certainty: certainty around higher-
quality, transparency in FCF, the ability to secure financing if needed, reasonable

valuations and strong moats that last beyond peak profitability. This is why we have
experienced a shift from the more highly cyclical semiconductors to the hyperscalers in
terms of positioning.
The market wants to see that AI spending remains robust but with more reasonable
expectations for 2027 and perhaps 2028. A reset of expectations by the leaders of
the buildout can go a long way to finalizing this “flush” stage, in our view. We remain
diversified Equity and are proud Bulls overall.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

S&P 500/Market-cap Weighted Index is a market-capitalization-weighted index that is widely regarded as the best single gauge of large-cap U.S. equities. The index includes 500 leading
companies and covers approximately 80% of available market capitalization
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is the equal-weight version of the widely-used S&P 500, in which each constituent company is given the same weight (approximately 0.2% each), rather than
weighting by market capitalization.

volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

Document 307: Construction rotates from infrastructure to housing and data centres (2026-11-25)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Construction rotates from infrastructure to housing and data centres (2026-11-25).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 18
Status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #construction
Construction rotates from infrastructure to housing and data
centres
By Pat Bustamante
Senior Economist, Westpac
14:36 November 25 2026 Share
Construction activity rose 0.9%qtr in Q3 and 5.8%yr. Construction activity remained solid,
driven by residential building (+3.1%qtr), non-residential building (+1.5%qtr, includes data
centres), partly offset by engineering construction (-0.9%qtr).

### Page 2

Construction activity rose 0.9%qtr in Q3 2026, taking annual growth to a strong 5.8%. The quarterly
increase was close to Westpac Economics’ 0.8%qtr forecast, but well above the 0.4%qtr market
consensus. Importantly, sizeable upward revisions to private construction lifted annual growth by
almost 2ppts. These revisions suggest the economy was somewhat stronger in 2025–26 than
previously thought, despite the same observed inflation outcomes.

What does this mean for the near-term outlook?
Overall, today’s data show construction activity continuing to expand at a solid pace, although there are
important offsetting forces beneath the headline result. Public infrastructure work remains soft and is
expected to ease further as major east-coast projects reach completion. This is being partly offset by
public non-residential construction, particularly health, schools and other social infrastructure.
At the same time, private construction remains strong. Residential construction is up 9.3% over the
year, the commercial building pipeline, which includes data centres, is around record highs, and the
renewable-energy investment pipeline remains substantial as we work towards the 82% renewable
electricity target by 2030.
Taken together, demand in the construction sector is likely to remain elevated in the near term, but its

### Page 3

composition is shifting. The pullback in public infrastructure is freeing up labour and other construction
capacity for housing, data centres, renewables and commercial projects. This rebalancing has
already been substantial, with our estimates suggesting the government footprint in the
construction industry declined by around 1.5ppts in 2025–26 (Government’s footprint plateaus as
services rise and construction retreats).
What does this mean for the National Accounts?

Today’s release is the first of a set of partials which will feed into next week’s September quarter 2026
National Accounts. Today’s numbers alone point to stronger growth in residential and new buildings
activity over the quarter and some upside for our Q3 estimate. However, with several important partial
indicators still to come, we will provide our full assessment in Friday’s GDP preview.
What about inflationary pressures and the impact of the data centre boom?

The spike in construction costs during the pandemic was largely synchronised across dwellings, non-
residential buildings and engineering construction. Today, however, cost pressures differ as demand
profiles across these segments diverge.
While rebounding in the quarter, engineering construction costs have been softer as major public
infrastructure programs wind down. In contrast, dwelling construction costs are accelerating as activity
in this segment picks up. Non-residential building costs appear to be going sideways, rather than

significantly reaccelerating, despite the strength in private investment, including data centres.

### Page 4

At 4.5%yr, aggregate construction cost growth is above what was recorded over 2018-19. This raises
questions about whether the strong demand in pockets like data centre investment are contributing to
broader inflationary pressures. For now, there is limited evidence of this happening. However,
construction cost deflators continue to firm in year-ended terms, suggesting that the disinflation
anticipated by the RBA may take longer to materialise.
How this plays out going forward depends on how much the substantial roll-off in public construction

activity makes room for the pickup in private residential and non-residential building work, particularly
data centres, and in private engineering construction associated with the renewable energy transition.
Total construction work yet to be done has risen to around 9.2% of nominal GDP in Q2 2026, compared
with an average of around 8.8% since 2022.
If that pipeline continues to build faster than public infrastructure releases capacity,
construction-sector cost pressures could prove more persistent than currently anticipated.

However, this is not our central case, as higher interest rates and ongoing cost increases are
likely to dampen private demand, while the public infrastructure pipeline continues to wind
down.

### Page 5

The detail
Residential construction outperformed, rising 3.1%qtr in Q3, following an upwardly revised 2.8%qtr
increase in Q2 2026 (previously estimated at 1.7%qtr). Growth was driven by a 3.9%qtr increase in
private residential construction, its strongest quarterly gain since June 2005, outside the COVID period.
In year-ended terms, residential construction is up 9.3%yr, the strongest growth rate since the
September quarter of 2016. This suggests the sector is responding to growing housing demand by

expanding output despite ongoing cost pressures. Some of this strength may also reflect the pullback
in public construction activity, which is freeing up labour and other resources for residential and non-
residential projects.
The strength in residential construction suggests the sector is responding more meaningfully to
Australia's housing shortage. However, with approvals likely to soften as higher interest rates bite and
demand moderates, the current pace of expansion may prove difficult to sustain.

### Page 6

Engineering construction (EC) declined 0.9%qtr in Q3 2026 but was 1.3%higher in year-ended
terms.
As foreshadowed in this month’s Market Outlook, the public infrastructure pipeline appears to have
passed its peak, as major transport projects across Australia’s east coast, particularly in NSW and
Victoria, move towards completion. Consistent with this, public engineering construction declined
3.0%year in Q3 but stabilised over the quarter to post a gain of 2.9%qtr. More broadly, the trend

remains weak, with activity falling in five of the past seven quarters.

### Page 7

In contrast, private engineering construction remains robust, up almost 5% over the year. However,
activity fell 3.7%qtr in Q3, suggesting some moderation may also be emerging in this segment. One
area of continued strength is electricity infrastructure, with the value of private work on electricity
generation and distribution rising by an estimated 10%qtr in seasonally adjusted and nominal terms,
largely reflecting renewable energy projects such as wind and solar developments.

Looking ahead, the private engineering pipeline remains supported by structural investment themes,
particularly the energy transition and nationwide rollout of data centres. However, constraints around
utility provision, grid connections and supporting infrastructure remain a key risk to the timely delivery
of these projects.
Non-residential building construction increased 1.5%qtr in Q3 2026, leaving activity 10.2% higher
over the year. Private-sector activity was mixed, declining 0.4%qtr, but remained 11% higher in year-

ended terms. Public activity also picked up sharply, rising 6.2%qtr to be 8.3% higher over the year,
partly reflecting investment in health and other facilities required to service a growing population.
Overall, non-residential construction remains strong, supported by two key investment themes: the
rollout of data centres in the private sector and increased public investment in health and other social
infrastructure, including in Queensland.

### Page 8

Although the contribution from individual projects is difficult to quantify, the surge in data centre
commencements during the second half of 2025 is likely supporting activity and should remain an
important source of construction demand over the second half of this year and beyond.

### Page 9

State detail
Construction activity increased across four of the six states in Q3, although outcomes varied
considerably.
South Australia recorded the strongest quarterly increase, rising 3.9%qtr, followed by NSW (+2.2%qtr)
and Victoria (+0.8%qtr). In contrast, activity declined in Western Australia (–1.9%qtr) and Queensland
(–1.2%qtr).
Despite the quarterly volatility, construction activity remained higher than a year ago across all states.
Tasmania recorded the strongest annual growth (+17.3%yr), followed by South Australia (+11.3%yr),

NSW (+8.1%yr), Western Australia (+6.4%yr), Victoria (+4.4%yr) and Queensland (+2.6%yr).

### Page 1

COP31 Preview: Cooperation Amid Fragmentation
COP31, taking place in Antalya, Türkiye from 9 to 20 November 2026, will test whether the ‘Implementation COP’ can
turn climate ambition into finance delivery amid heightened geopolitical tensions. Corporates and investors should
watch for signals that could shape policy and capital allocation, including commitments to grid investment,
electrification, adaptation and carbon markets. We also expect AI’s climate potential and rising electricity demand to

Deutsche Bank
Research
Debbie Jones | Andrew Dixon
S U S T A I N A B I L I T Y  | T H E M A T I C  R E S E A R C H  | O C T O B E R  2 0 2 6
The report outlines the top 10 focus topics, discussions and outcomes for COP31 (see pages 1-3 for a summary)

### Page 2

C O P 3 1  P r ev iew  |  TO P  1 0  (1  o f 3 )
Antalya  - 10 things to watch for at COP31
2 Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Preview  |  October 2026
From 9 to 20 November, ~100,000 participants and nearly 200 Parties are expected at COP31 in Antalya, Türkiye. This is an ‘Implementation COP’ and will focus on climate finance delivery for
mitigation and adaptation commitments. Yes, there will be geopolitical tensions and competitiveness concerns. But, for corporates and investors, the discussions will shape how private capital is

mobilised, and how policies will affect de-risking efforts. We expect electrification efforts will feature prominently, driven by energy security concerns as well as by rising AI-driven power
demand. Furthermore, AI’s ability to help tackle climate change has the potential to play a bigger role in shaping the agenda of future COPs.
01
TÜRKIYE HOSTS, AUSTRALIA LEADS
NEGOTIATIONS
Türkiye and Australia must stay aligned
to drive progress
See page 6
– As host, Türkiye’s Murat Kurum will preside over COP31 and its Action Agenda, which is intended to build cooperation between

willing participants to drive climate action. Australia’s Chris Bowen will run the formal negotiations that will seek consensus (or
lack of objection) on finance delivery and existing climate commitments. Feedback following pre-COP meetings on the
arrangement has been broadly positive, but concern remains over how any potential disagreement will be handled.
– This structure is the result of a compromise after both countries bid to host. Both countries want COP31 to focus on climate

finance implementation and clean-energy growth, however, they remain coal-reliant (in different ways) with no national coal
phase-out date, a criticism likely to continue from climate advocates at COP.
02
CLIMATE FINANCE
Success at COP31 requires notable
progress on mobilising existing
commitments
See page 7
– For COP31 to be successful it will need to deliver agreements to mobilise and channel climate finance. For context, at COP29 in
2024 it was agreed (as part of formal negotiations) that developed nations would commit $300bn in climate finance annually

by 2035, with a call for $1.3tn from all sources. At present, more credible plans are needed to secure existing commitments
and attract broader capital. Note the EU has already stated it will not make new finance pledges at COP31.
– Article 9 contains the main financing provisions of the Paris Agreement and has driven much debate during COP talks.
Developing countries want focus on public funding from developed governments (Article 9.1), while developed countries prefer

to discuss Article 9 in a broader context. This allows private capital to be included. COP30 launched a two-year work
programme on Article 9, but June’s Bonn talks stalled, so COP31 meetings will attempt to reach agreement.
03
ADAPTATION & RESILIENCE
Implications of a 1.5°C overshoot point
to more adaptation financing
See pages 8–9
– The UN has warned that an overshoot of the Paris Agreement’s 1.5°C goal is now unavoidable, at least temporarily. That has

dampened morale for climate supporters ahead of COP but, at the same time, it has drawn more attention to the topic of
adaptation finance, which is set to be on the formal negotiating agenda. Parties appear deadlocked over whether the COP30
goal to triple adaptation finance by 2035 (potential annual target of $120bn) should be explicitly referenced in the final COP31
decision on the Global Goal on Adaptation (GGA). This is important, as anchoring it to the GGA could signal finance flows.

– The EU Commission proposed the Climate Resilience law on 27 October. It needs Parliament and Council approval, but if
approved, member states will be required to run national climate-risk assessments based on a 3°C-by-2100 warming scenario
(i.e. the EU is planning for overshoot). Members would also need to adopt resilience plans, including details of costs and
funding, with The Commission estimating the EU needs €70bn a year for adaptation to 2050, vs. €29bn of current spend.

### Page 3

Antalya  - 10 things to watch for at COP31
3 Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Preview  |  October 2026
04
ELECTRIFICATION
Türkiye to champion voluntary
electrification commitments
See page 10
– Türkiye's voluntary Action Agenda includes a ‘35-by-35’ target to lift electricity’s share of final energy use from ~23% to 35% by
2035, alongside waste, building energy intensity and circularity targets. This sits outside the formal negotiations and commits
only those that volunteer. China and the EU have voiced support, and the timing is right given global energy-security concerns,

though some question the emissions impact if fossil fuels are used to generate the additional electricity.
06
TRADE TENSIONS
Trade’s inclusion in the provisional
agenda is contentious, with the
potential for disagreement over trade to
stall progress on wider agreements
See page 12
– The role of trade at COP is contentious, but China wants it discussed. On 21 October, Vice Minister Li Gao called ‘unilateralism
and protectionism’ a ‘new problem’ undermining collective climate efforts. Specifically, he wants COP31 to discuss the free

flow of green technology. This was widely read as being aimed at US and EU tariffs on Chinese cleantech.
– Despite a COP30 agreement to create three annual trade dialogues in 2026–28 (the first formal space under the UNFCCC to
discuss trade) away from COP, developing countries are still pushing for trade to be a formal negotiating item in Antalya, and it
is on the provisional agenda. They argue that climate-related trade measures such as the CBAM burden their exporters and

question whether they are compatible with UNFCCC principles. The EU meanwhile claims they help prevent carbon leakage,
with developed countries preferring trade discussions to focus on technical information-sharing without duplicating the WTO.
C O P 3 1  P r ev iew  |  TO P  1 0  (2  o f 3 )
07
GEOPOLITICS & MULTILATERALISM
Consensus under strain, but ‘coalitions
of the willing’ are rising
See pages 13–14
– Geopolitical fragmentation and the US exit from the Paris Agreement make consensus, historically central to COP decision-

making, harder among nearly 200 nations. This has encouraged more voluntary action and “coalitions of the willing” to pursue
more ambitious goals. If these strategies deliver more effective progress, their approach will shape future COPs.
05
FOSSIL FUELS
Phase-out likely not on the formal
agenda, but workarounds are emerging
See page 11
– Brazil’s COP30 presidency is to present a fossil fuel transition roadmap separately, outside the UN formal negotiating process.

For perspective, COP28 in Dubai reached consensus on “transitioning away from fossil fuels in energy systems”, but at COP30
in Belém (2025) calls from more than 80 countries for a transition roadmap faced opposition from other nations, so it was
omitted from the final decision and moved to a separate track.
– Santa Marta is a second workaround for the lack of formal agreement on a transition roadmap. The Colombia- and
Netherlands-led process is a ‘coalition of the willing’ of 57 participants (excluding the US, China, India and Russia) agreeing to

voluntary action to move away from fossil fuels faster than consensus allows. At COP31, Colombia and the Netherlands want
to bring the Santa Marta outcomes into the summit. The format is not yet settled, but the Australian and Turkish presidencies
have said they are open to it. The process then heads to a second conference in Tuvalu, co-hosted with Ireland, in 2027.

### Page 4

09
NATURE & CARBON CREDITS
Efforts to boost demand and build
credibility to help scale carbon markets
See page 16
– Carbon credit discussions will centre on government policies to boost demand and corporate engagement, with much of the
formal UN architecture now in place. If carbon markets can scale, the benefits would be wide-ranging (e.g. supporting
emissions targets and providing finance for developing countries). Credibility and greenwashing concerns remain key risks,

however; we expect prices to diverge in favour of high-integrity credits.
– The Tropical Forest Forever Facility (TFFF), launched at COP30, plans a fixed-income portfolio to reward verified forest
conservation. It now has ~$7.8bn of sponsor capital against a $10bn end-2026 target. In our view, fresh COP31 commitments
would signal appetite to fund nature conservation and protection.
10
PACIFIC, OCEANS & SEA-LEVEL RISE
Australia to bring Pacific priorities into

focus
See page 17
– Australia asked for a Pacific pre-COP in return for ceding the hosting. It has promised to keep championing Pacific interests at
COP31. The Taku Pakasoa Declaration was adopted at that pre-COP on 6 October. ‘Taku Pakasoa’ is Tuvaluan for strength
found in unity and collective action. It is a joint call to keep 1.5°C alive, calling for action on climate finance, resilience and
oceans. Its intention is to give the Pacific a shared position going into COP31 and signal its policy priorities to other blocs.

Other countries are invited to sign on. The climate-ocean nexus will also be a COP31 focus, including investable solutions.
COP31 should indicate what COP can deliver in a more fragmented geopolitical world. Key questions? Can the current structure help mobilise meaningful finance and investment, and do
smaller coalitions and voluntary commitments deliver meaningful outcomes? Answers here could shape future COP agendas.  Looking ahead, Ethiopia will host COP32 in 2027. There, we

expect a continued focus on delivering on commitments and improving access to climate finance, reflecting African priorities. COP33 then falls to Asia-Pacific in 2028, with China
reportedly considering a bid and South Korea potentially interested.
We should note that the second Global Stocktake, the Paris Agreement’s five-yearly review of collective progress, begins at COP31 and concludes at COP33, with a technical assessment
(2027) informing the final negotiations. Its outcome should inform countries’ next national climate plans (NDCs, their emissions-reduction pledges), due in 2030.

08
AI & CLIMATE
AI’s power demand in focus, with the
Antalya pledge calling for low emissions
infrastructure
See page 15
– The Antalya AI Pledge invites governments and companies to commit voluntarily to sustainable infrastructure and
environmental transparency. Its call for zero- and low-emissions energy infrastructure leaves room for nuclear power,
prompting discussion of its role in meeting data-centre demand alongside renewables. While we see binding commitments as

unlikely, watch for corporate power commitments and technology partnerships.
– We expect AI’s climate potential and rising electricity demand to feature prominently at COP31, prompting debate over data-
centre energy use. We also expect AI to help delegations manage COP’s heavy paperwork, from reviewing negotiating texts
and submissions to comparing successive draft decisions. This should help negotiating teams prepare more effectively and
make decisions faster, particularly smaller delegations with limited staff and resources.
